People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I mean… it’s the routing system. It’s been marking where I go, what I do, sending things through the scanners. Feels like it’s trying to get me pushed out because I’ve been there so long.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system's actions are definitely real, or could they be your imagination?\n\nPatient: It’s real. I know how it sounds, but it’s not just in my head. The system’s been marking my moves and sending things through the scanners… like it’s trying to push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything else going on besides the routing system that's trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: No… not really anything else. It’s mainly the routing system. The scanners, the way it marks where I go… that’s what’s pushing me out. Because I’ve been there a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard the routing system or scanners talk to you directly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Not, uh, not like a regular conversation. But I’ll be in my garage, everything shut off, and I can hear dispatch voices… clear. Calling my operator number. Saying I’m dangerous, or that security’s coming.\n\nDoctor: Do you still hear the dispatch voices when you're not in your garage?\n\nPatient: Mostly in the garage… that’s where it happens clear. With everything shut off, I still hear dispatch calling my operator number, saying I’m dangerous or security’s coming. Outside there… I don’t like to say. It’s not as clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe the routing system is trying to push you out when you're not hearing the dispatch voices?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean… the voices aren’t the main part. Even when it’s quiet, I still know the routing system is marking my moves. The scanners send it through. It’s been trying to push me out because I’ve been there too long, one of the old hands.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods, even brief ones, where you didn't think the routing system was out to get you?\n\nPatient: No… not really. It’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods, even brief ones, where you didn't think the routing system was out to get you?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I might try not to think about it for a bit, but I don’t stop knowing it’s doing that. The scanners, the routes… it’s been marking me, pushing me out because I’ve been there so long.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think the routing system was trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: No. Not even for a day, I don’t think. I try to keep my head down, but I still know it’s marking me through those scanners… trying to edge me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe in anything else that most people would find hard to believe or think is not true?\n\nPatient: Just that, really… the system at the warehouse. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… things at work haven’t felt right. The routing system at the warehouse, it’s not just a computer anymore. I know it’s been tracking my moves, sending messages through the scanners… trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone or something is intentionally trying to harm you or your job at the warehouse?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… it’s the routing system. It’s been marking where I go, what I do, sending things through the scanners. Feels like it’s trying to get me pushed out because I’ve been there so long.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the routing system's actions are definitely real, or could they be your imagination?\n\nPatient: It’s real. I know how it sounds, but it’s not just in my head. The system’s been marking my moves and sending things through the scanners… like it’s trying to push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything else going on besides the routing system that's trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: No… not really anything else. It’s mainly the routing system. The scanners, the way it marks where I go… that’s what’s pushing me out. Because I’ve been there a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard the routing system or scanners talk to you directly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Not, uh, not like a regular conversation. But I’ll be in my garage, everything shut off, and I can hear dispatch voices… clear. Calling my operator number. Saying I’m dangerous, or that security’s coming.\n\nDoctor: Do you still hear the dispatch voices when you're not in your garage?\n\nPatient: Mostly in the garage… that’s where it happens clear. With everything shut off, I still hear dispatch calling my operator number, saying I’m dangerous or security’s coming. Outside there… I don’t like to say. It’s not as clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe the routing system is trying to push you out when you're not hearing the dispatch voices?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean… the voices aren’t the main part. Even when it’s quiet, I still know the routing system is marking my moves. The scanners send it through. It’s been trying to push me out because I’ve been there too long, one of the old hands.\n\nDoctor: Can anything convince you that the routing system is not trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what would convince me. It’s… it’s not just a feeling. I know it’s been marking my moves through the scanners, trying to push me out because I’ve been there too long. So no, not really.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like the routing system is trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: About… a year and a half. Maybe closer to a year and eight months. It’s been going on a while.\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts about the routing system trying to push you out ever changed or wavered since they started?\n\nPatient: Not really. It’s been pretty much the same. I mean… I know the system’s been marking my moves through the scanners, trying to push me out. I don’t see that changing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't believe the routing system was trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Once I saw what it was doing with the scanners, it’s been hard to see it any other way. It’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any periods, even brief ones, where you didn't think the routing system was out to get you?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I might try not to think about it for a bit, but I don’t stop knowing it’s doing that. The scanners, the routes… it’s been marking me, pushing me out because I’ve been there so long.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think the routing system was trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: No. Not even for a day, I don’t think. I try to keep my head down, but I still know it’s marking me through those scanners… trying to edge me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe in anything else that most people would find hard to believe or think is not true?\n\nPatient: Just that, really… the system at the warehouse. People say it’s just routing and scanners, but I know it’s marking my moves. Sending things through the scanners to push me out… because I’ve been there a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts like this about other things besides the routing system at any point in your life?\n\nPatient: No… not really other things. It’s mostly the warehouse system. The routing, the scanners… that’s where it is. It knows my movements there, sends things a certain way. I don’t have that with everything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other beliefs that you held strongly, despite most people around you thinking they were not true?\n\nPatient: No… not other beliefs. It’s that warehouse routing system. I know people don’t believe me, but it’s been marking where I move, using the scanners… trying to push me out because I’ve been there so long.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the routing system trying to push you out lasted for your entire life or just started more recently?\n\nPatient: Not my whole life. It started… more recent. Maybe a little over two years ago—about two years and a few months. After they changed the routing system and put us on those rotating overnight shifts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean… it’s the routing system. It’s been marking where I go, what I do, sending things through the scanners. I know how it sounds, but it’s not just in my head. The system’s been marking my moves and sending things through the scanners… like it’s trying to push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything else going on besides the routing system that's trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: No… not really anything else. It’s mainly the routing system. The scanners, the way it marks where I go… that’s what’s pushing me out. Because I’ve been there a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard the routing system or scanners talk to you directly?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Not, uh, not like a regular conversation. Saying I’m dangerous, or that security’s coming.\n\nDoctor: Do you still hear the dispatch voices when you're not in your garage?\n\nPatient: Mostly in the garage… that’s where it happens clear. The scanners, the routes… it’s been marking me, pushing me out because I’ve been there so long.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even if just for a day, where you didn't think the routing system was trying to push you out?\n\nPatient: No. Not even for a day, I don’t think.
